Well... um... yes, I suppose it has spilled over a little bit outside of work. I really try to leave it at the door, but it's been hard. I actually used to be very involved in our local community theater—I love helping with the sets and costumes—but lately... I've just had to pull back. And because I've lost my patience with the trainees a few times, management decided it's best if I don't mentor the younger inspectors anymore. Which is fair, I completely understand why they did it, I just... I hate that I let them down. I'm usually so dependable. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making myself sound completely awful... I really am trying my best to fix this.

Um... well, I really try not to complain about every little thing, you know? I think at my age, and working on my feet as an inspector, a little muscle tension is just part of the job. And I really do try my absolute best to stay focused during my shifts.

Um... well, no, I wouldn't really say I'm a worrier. I mean, I think it's normal to care about doing a good job and making sure things are done right, especially in my line of work where safety is so important. I just try to be thorough, you know?

and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.\n  manifestation: I don’t trust that plant or the people running it anymore, and some days I feel like I’m damaged goods who can’t even do the job I spent my life getting good at.\n- key: S073\n  name: Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame\n  description: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.\n  manifestation: I keep thinking if I had pushed harder about the safety checks or stopped that run when it didn’t feel right, maybe he wouldn’t have gotten hurt so bad.\n- key: S077\n  name: Irritability_Aggression\n  description: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.\n  manifestation: I’m here because I keep snapping at people, like at that safety meeting when I started yelling before I could stop myself, and now the younger inspectors act scared to ask me anything.\n- key: S079\n  name: Hypervigilance\n  description: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.\n  manifestation: Even in the break room or at rehearsal, I’m watching doors, listening for sudden machine noises, and checking who’s moving behind me because my body won’t believe I’m safe.\n- key: Um... well... I guess maybe a little bit? I really don't want to sound like I'm not doing my job, because I am. I still go to work every day and do my best. But... And... well, if people bring up the accident, I really try to just change the subject. I don't want to bring down the mood or make anyone uncomfortable by talking about it. It's better to just keep things positive, right? 

I also... I used to really love going to these local industrial-history talks. It sounds a bit nerdy, I know, but I found them so interesting. But lately, I just... I stopped going. They show all these pictures of old machinery and... it just kind of sets me off, I guess. I get all tense, and I don't want to be a bother to the people sitting next to me if I start acting jumpy. 

I hope it doesn't make me sound weak or anything, avoiding those things. I just want to keep the peace and not be a burden. Is it okay that I handle it that way? I'm just trying to do what's best so I don't upset anyone else.

Oh... um... I really hate to dwell on it, because I wasn't the one who got physically hurt, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm making it all about me, or being dramatic. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yes, I... I think I did mention that just a moment ago, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much or being confusing. But yes, that's exactly what happens. 

It's like... if I hear a loud clank from one of the utility carts going by, or if I catch that specific smell of hot oil... it's just awful. It's like I'm not in my current spot anymore. I'm suddenly right back in that bay, and I can see my coworker by the machine, and it feels exactly like it's happening all over again right in front of me. 

I know I'm perfectly safe, and I try so hard to just shake it off and keep working so I don't cause a fuss. I really don't want anyone to think I'm losing my mind or that I can't handle my job. It just takes over for a minute, you know? I hope it's okay that I feel that way... does that make sense to you?

Um... well... I guess I do, a little bit? I mean, I really try not to be jumpy, because I know it probably looks silly to other people. I don't want anyone to think I'm paranoid or anything like that. 

But... even when I'm just sitting in the break room, or... or even when I'm at my choir rehearsal on the weekends, I find myself doing it. I try to stop, I really do, but I just... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... um, well... I really try not to let it interfere with my duties, I promise. I don't want to be difficult or make extra work for anyone. But... I suppose I do, yes. 

I actually go out of my way now so I don't have to pass that specific inspection bay. I take the long way around the floor, which I hope my supervisor doesn't think means I'm wasting time... it just helps me keep my composure so I can do a good job. And... well, I really won't talk about the accident if I can help it. If someone brings it up, I try to just politely change the subject. I don't want to bring the mood down or make anyone else uncomfortable, you know?

I also... well, this feels a bit silly to admit, but I used to really enjoy going to these local industrial-history talks. I actually quit going to them recently. The pictures they show of the old machinery... they just set me off too much now. I feel awful for missing them, but I just don't want to risk getting upset in front of people and making a scene. I hope it's okay that I'm avoiding those things? I just really want to handle this the right way without being a burden.

I suppose I do go out of my way to not walk past the main inspection bay anymore. It's just... it's easier to take the long way around, you know? I don't want to cause a scene or get upset in front of everyone. \n\nAnd... well, if people bring up the accident, I really try to just change the subject. I don't want to bring down the mood or make anyone uncomfortable by talking about it. It's better to just keep things positive, right? \n\nI also... I just want to keep the peace and not be a burden. Is it okay that I handle it that way? I'm just trying to do what's best so I don't upset anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the accident you mentioned? I don't want to sound like I'm making it all about me, or being dramatic. It was just a really bad accident with one of the machines, and my coworker... well, I was right there doing my inspection when it happened. \n\nBut to answer your question... I guess I do get a little upset sometimes. I try so hard to just push through it and do my job, I really do. But... well, sometimes just a loud clank from a utility cart, or... or if I catch that smell of hot oil... it just puts me right back in that bay. It’s like... I see my coworker by the machine, and it feels like it’s happening in front of me all over again. \n\nI know it probably sounds silly, because I know I'm safe where I am. I really try to just keep my head down, take a breath, and not let it show so I don't bother anyone else on the floor. I hope that's an okay answer? I just feel so foolish when my mind does that... is it normal to get spooked by little things like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're actually reliving the accident, like it's happening all over again in your mind, even when you know you're safe?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I... I think I did mention that just a moment ago, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much or being confusing. But yes, that's exactly what happens. \n\nIt's like... if I hear a loud clank from one of the utility carts going by, or if I catch that specific smell of hot oil... it's just awful. It's like I'm not in my current spot anymore. I'm suddenly right back in that bay, and I can see my coworker by the machine, and it feels exactly like it's happening all over again right in front of me. \n\nI know I'm perfectly safe, and I try so hard to just shake it off and keep working so I don't cause a fuss. I really don't want anyone to think I'm losing my mind or that I can't handle my job. It just takes over for a minute, you know? I hope it's okay that I feel that way... does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being easily startled or constantly on guard, even in situations where you know you’re safe?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I do, a little bit? I mean, I really try not to be jumpy, because I know it probably looks silly to other people. I don't want anyone to think I'm paranoid or anything like that. \n\nBut... even when I'm just sitting in the break room, or... or even when I'm at my choir rehearsal on the weekends, I find myself doing it. I try to stop, I really do, but I just... I catch myself always watching the doors, you know? And I'm always listening so hard for any sudden machine noises, even when I'm nowhere near the floor. And... I'm constantly checking who's moving behind me. \n\nIt's just like... no matter how much I tell myself I'm fine, my body just won't believe that I'm safe. I really hope that doesn't sound too crazy. I try so hard to hide it so I don't make anyone else nervous. Is it okay that I'm still reacting like that? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting to things.\n\nDoctor: Do you go out of your way to avoid people, places, or things that remind you of the accident?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, well... I really try not to let it interfere with my duties, I promise. I don't want to be difficult or make extra work for anyone. But... I suppose I do, yes. \n\nI actually go out of my way now so I don't have to pass that specific inspection bay. I take the long way around the floor, which I hope my supervisor doesn't think means I'm wasting time... it just helps me keep my composure so I can do a good job.
